### P1-T08 Provider contract discovery - DOC_CONFIRMED
- Current Zernio Analytics Webhooks documentation identifies `analytics.synced` as the Analytics sync event.
- Current request-body schema requires top-level `id`, `event`, `account`, `sync`, and `timestamp`; optional top-level fields include `profiles` and `test`.
- Documented account attribution fields are `accountId`, `profileId`, `platform`, and `username`.
- Documented sync metadata fields are `syncedAt` and `postsUpdated`.
- Current schema text describes the event as poll-driven, roughly hourly per connected account, after a successful analytics sync cycle; this wording is documentation guidance, not a Provider SLA.
- Current request-body schema describes `analytics.synced` as a trigger rather than metrics transport and says metrics/cursor are not expected in that event. Current introductory prose on the same page contains conflicting cursor wording, so this is recorded as documentation drift and live Provider observation remains authoritative.
- Documentation recommends a dedicated Analytics webhook subscription because of event volume/shared failure-count concerns.
- Documentation evidence is `DOC_CONFIRMED` only; no live Provider payload fact is claimed by P1-T08 yet.

## Phase 1 Rules
- Work one Task at a time.
- Provider Behavior Tests remain separate from System Integration Tests.
- No additional V1 Vertical/Feature without explicit Scope Change.
- Basic cost protection before first paid API call.

Unit: Type `forking`; PIDFile `/run/nginx.pid`; ExecStartPre `/usr/sbin/nginx -t -q -g 'daemon on; master_process on;'`; ExecStart `/usr/sbin/nginx -g 'daemon on; master_process on;'`; ExecReload uses `-s reload`; Restart `no`; no drop-ins observed.
- Bounded journal proves exact failure: after reboot, nginx started at `10:11:26 UTC`; ExecStart PID `843` repeatedly failed to bind `0.0.0.0:80` and `[::]:80` with `98: Address already in use` from `10:11:27` through `10:11:29 UTC`, then `still could not bind()`, and systemd recorded exit status `1/FAILURE`.

### P1-T08-F11-RCV01 - live receiver/harness compatibility preflight result (2026-10-21)

`P1-T08-F11-RCV01 = EXECUTED / AWAITING TEAM LEADER REVIEW`.

Classification = `TEMPORARY_NODE_RECEIVER_HANDOFF_REQUIRED`.

Question A — Can the current static VPS receiver be used unchanged by the accepted F10 live runner while preserving the accepted readiness/HMAC/event-ID/target/dedup/passive-observation/classification semantics? **NO**.

Accepted harness evidence: `p1-t08-f10-runner.ts` creates one per-run secret/marker and starts `startP1T08Receiver(...)` on loopback `127.0.0.1` at the approved configured local port, using the public receiver URL pathname. The public readiness probe performs two HTTPS `POST` requests to the exact public URL, first with `x-aimm-p1-t08-readiness: <marker>` and then `x-aimm-p1-t08-readiness-confirmed: <marker>`; it requires status `401` plus `x-aimm-p1-t08-receiver: <marker>` from the receiver, with the second confirmation also matching. The accepted Node receiver treats those requests as non-event readiness traffic and keeps in-memory readiness counters. No readiness POST was executed in RCV01.

The accepted Node receiver verifies Zernio HMAC-SHA256 using the per-run webhook secret, requires header/body event-ID equality and header/body event-name equality, validates expected profile/account/platform, rejects explicit foreign/wrong-platform/unconfirmed targets with bounded status semantics, tracks logical duplicate event IDs, records structured delivery evidence, exposes `snapshot()`, and provides passive `waitForAnalytics(...)`. `runP1T08F10(...)` directly depends on that same `P1T08ReceiverController` for observation evidence and deterministic A/B/C/D/E classification.

Current static VPS receiver evidence: `/opt/marketly-r02/receiver.py` SHA-256 = `f2851eac800b417ec870083f7acb69f0fa231fdea645c96b328736486fa7dcb9`; systemd unit SHA-256 = `c0d3c948068ebbd5c315e88d6a305e3dc2a66a18f497470a3ef88c547c9ff1d7`; MarketlyAI Nginx config SHA-256 = `7a54f35600a606b3e774c70a3bb03f3b01b4b2ec34851a79ea4daa51bc2f1a06`. The Python receiver is fixed to `127.0.0.1:8765` and exact path `/p1-t08-f11/analytics-synced`. GET/HEAD on the exact path return `200`; POST reads the body, persists secure body+metadata evidence, and returns `204`; unsupported methods are `405` on the exact path. It does **not** implement the F10 readiness marker protocol, does not calculate/verify the per-run HMAC, does not validate Zernio event/header/body IDs or expected target, does not deduplicate logical events, and cannot provide the in-memory `P1T08ReceiverController` state required by the accepted F10 harness. It is a durable evidence collector, not the accepted live F10 receiver controller. A readiness POST sent to it would be captured as evidence rather than acknowledged with the required `401` marker handshake, so RCV01 intentionally sent no POST.

Question B — Does the accepted design require the public exact route temporarily to reach the local Node receiver used by the F10 runner? **YES**. The runner starts that receiver before calling `probeP1T08VpsReceiverHttps(...)`, and readiness can only pass if the public exact URL reaches the same Node receiver instance carrying the generated readiness marker and in-memory controller state.

The accepted Nginx exact route already proxies unchanged to `http://127.0.0.1:8765`, so no Nginx config edit is required for the proposed handoff.
